AND repo = ? AND number = ?\n\t\t ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1`,\n\t\towner, repo, number,\n\t).Scan(&sha)\n\tswitch {\n\tcase err == sql.ErrNoRows:\n\t\treturn \"\", false, nil\n\tcase err != nil:\n\t\treturn \"\", false, fmt.Errorf(\"latest round sha: %w\", err)\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn sha, true, nil\n\t}\n}\n\n// InsertToken stores a hashed installation token (ADR-0003)."
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/internal/hub/webhook.go",
  "content": "// Package hub implements the Caw Hub's HTTP-facing logic: webhook ingest,\n// signature verification, delivery dedupe, Round bucketing, signal extraction,\n// and the get_pending endpoint.\npackage hub\n\nimport (\n\t\"crypto/hmac\"\n\t\"crypto/sha256\"\n\t\"encoding/hex\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"io\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/github\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/settle\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store\"\n)\n\n// maxBody caps the webhook payload we read (GitHub's documented ceiling is 25 MiB).\nconst maxBody = 25 << 20\n\n// Hub holds the dependencies for handling webhooks and serving pending items.\ntype Hub struct {\n\tstore   *store.Store\n\tsecret  []byte\n\tsettler *settle.Engine // may be nil (e.g. in unit tests of pure ingest)\n}\n\n// New constructs a Hub. settler may be nil, in which case settles are not scheduled.\nfunc New(st *store.Store, secret []byte, settler *settle.Engine) *Hub {\n\treturn &Hub{store: st, secret: secret, settler: settler}\n}\n\n// VerifySignature reports whether sigHeader (\"sha256=<hex>\") is a valid\n// HMAC-SHA256 of payload under secret. The comparison is constant-time.\nfunc VerifySignature(secret, payload []byte, sigHeader string) bool {\n\tconst prefix = \"sha256=\"\n\tif !strings.HasPrefix(sigHeader, prefix) {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\twant, err := hex.DecodeString(sigHeader[len(prefix):])\n\tif err != nil || len(want) != sha256.Size {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tmac := hmac.New(sha256.New, secret)\n\tmac.Write(payload)\n\treturn hmac.Equal(want, mac.Sum(nil))\n}\n\n// HandleWebhook is the POST /webhooks/github handler: verify, dedupe, then ingest.\nfunc (h *Hub) HandleWebhook(c *gin.Context) {\n\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(c.Request.Body, maxBody))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"read body\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif len(h.secret) == 0 || !VerifySignature(h.secret, body, c.GetHeader(\"X-Hub-Signature-256\")) {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusUnauthorized, \"invalid signature\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tevent := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Event\")\n\tif delivery := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Delivery\"); delivery != \"\" {\n\t\tisNew, err := h.store.SeenDelivery(delivery, event)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"dedupe delivery %s: %v\", delivery, err)\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"dedupe\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !isNew {\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusOK, \"duplicate\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tvar env github.Envelope\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(body, &env); err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"parse payload\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.ingest(event, env); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"ingest %s: %v\", event, err)\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"ingest\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tc.String(http.StatusAccepted, \"accepted\")\n}\n\n// nonFailing check conclusions that should not raise a checks signal.\nvar passingConclusions = map[string]bool{\"success\": true, \"neutral\": true, \"skipped\": true}\n\n// ingest records the Round, extracts any signal, and arms the settle timer.\nfunc (h *Hub) ingest(event string, env github.Envelope) error {\n\towner := env.Repository.Owner.Login\n\trepo := env.Repository.Name\n\tif owner == \"\" || repo == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tswitch event {\n\tcase \"check_suite\":\n\t\tcs := env.CheckSuite\n\t\tif cs == nil || env.Action != \"completed\" || len(cs.PullRequests) == 0 {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tnumber, sha := cs.PullRequests[0].Number, cs.HeadSHA\n\t\tif err := h.store.RecordRound(owner, repo, number, sha); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !passingConclusions[cs.Conclusion] && cs.Conclusion != \"\" {\n\t\t\tsource := cs.App.Name\n\t\t\tif source == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tsource = \"checks\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif err := h.store.AddSignal(store.Signal{\n\t\t\t\tOwner: owner, Repo: repo, Number: number, SHA: sha,\n\t\t\t\tSignalType: \"checks\", Source: source,\n\t\t\t\tExternalID: fmt.Sprintf(\"suite-%d\", cs.ID),\n\t\t\t\tSeverity:   cs.Conclusion, Body: cs.Conclusion,\n\t\t\t}); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\th.touch(owner, repo, number, sha)\n\n\tcase \"pull_request\":\n\t\tif env.PullRequest == nil || env.PullRequest.Head.SHA == \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn h.store.RecordRound(owner, repo, env.PullRequest.Number, env.PullRequest.Head.SHA)\n\n\tcase \"pull_request_review\":\n\t\tif env.PullRequest == nil || env.Review == nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn h.comment(owner, repo, env.PullRequest.Number, env.PullRequest.Head.SHA,\n\t\t\tenv.Sender.Login, fmt.Sprintf(\"review-%d\", env.Review.ID), env.Review.Body)\n\n\tcase \"pull_request_review_comment\":\n\t\tif env.PullRequest == nil || env.Comment == nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn h.comment(owner, repo, env.PullRequest.Number, env.PullRequest.Head.SHA,\n\t\t\tenv.Sender.Login, fmt.Sprintf(\"rc-%d\", env.Comment.ID), env.Comment.Body)\n\n\tcase \"issue_comment\":\n\t\tif env.Issue == nil || env.Comment == nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tsha, ok, err := h.store.LatestRoundSHA(owner, repo, env.Issue.Number)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn nil // no known Round yet; nothing to attach the comment to\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn h.comment(owner, repo, env.Issue.Number, sha,\n\t\t\tenv.Sender.Login, fmt.Sprintf(\"ic-%d\", env.Comment.ID), env.Comment.Body)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// comment records a comments-type signal for a Round and arms the settle timer.\nfunc (h *Hub) comment(owner, repo string, number int, sha, source, externalID, body string) error {\n\tif sha == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif source == \"\" {\n\t\tsource = \"comment\"\n\t}\n\tif err := h.store.RecordRound(owner, repo, number, sha); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif err := h.store.AddSignal(store.Signal{\n\t\tOwner: owner, Repo: repo, Number: number, SHA: sha,\n\t\tSignalType: \"comments\", Source: source, ExternalID: externalID, Body: body,\n\t}); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\th.touch(owner, repo, number, sha)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (h *Hub) touch(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) {\n\tif h.settler != nil {\n\t\th.settler.Touch(owner, repo, number, sha)\n\t}\n}\n\n// HandlePending serves GET /pending: all current pending items (ADR-0006).\n// The consumer decides which to act on; each item carries its SHA, PR state,\n// and timestamp for relevance filtering.\nfunc (h *Hub) HandlePending(c *gin.Context) {\n\titems, err := h.store.ListPending()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"list pending: %v\", err)\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"pending\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tc.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{\"items\": items})\n}\n"
}

  "content": "// Package server wires the Hub's HTTP routes onto a Gin engine.\npackage server\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/auth\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/hub\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/settle\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/sse\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store\"\n)\n\n// New builds the Gin engine with all routes wired. The SSE and pending routes\n// are gated by installation-token auth (ADR-0003); the SSE route is registered\n// without buffering middleware so the held connection streams (ADR-0001).\nfunc New(st *store.Store, sseHub *sse.Hub, engine *settle.Engine, secret []byte) *gin.Engine {\n\tr := gin.New()\n\tr.Use(gin.Logger(), gin.Recovery())\n\n\th := hub.New(st, secret, engine)\n\tr.POST(\"/webhooks/github\", h.HandleWebhook)\n\tr.GET(\"/healthz\", func(c *gin.Context) { c.String(http.StatusOK, \"ok\") })\n\n\tauthMW := auth.Required(st)\n\tr.GET(\"/pending\", authMW, h.HandlePending)\n\tr.GET(\"/sse/:owner/:repo/:number\", authMW, sseHub.Handler(sseKey))\n\n\treturn r\n}\n\n// sseKey derives the PR subscription key from the request path; it must match\n// settle.PRKey's format (owner/repo#number).\nfunc sseKey(c *gin.Context) string {\n\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"%s/%s#%s\", c.Param(\"owner\"), c.Param(\"repo\"), c.Param(\"number\"))\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/cmd/hub/main.go",
  "content": "// Command hub is the Caw Hub: it ingests GitHub webhooks, compiles PR feedback\n// into summaries, fans them out to Watchers over SSE, and stores orphaned\n// feedback as pending items.\n//\n// Subcommand: `hub mint-token <installation_id> [org]` prints a new raw token.\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"os/signal\"\n\t\"syscall\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/auth\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/config\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/server\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/settle\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/sse\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store\"\n)\n\nfunc main() {\n\tcfg := config.Load()\n\n\tst, err := store.Open(cfg.DatabasePath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Fatalf(\"open store: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = st.Close() }()\n\n\tif len(os.Args) > 1 && os.Args[1] == \"mint-token\" {\n\t\tif err := mintToken(st, os.Args[2:]); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Fatalf(\"mint-token: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif cfg.GitHubWebhookSecret == \"\" {\n\t\tlog.Println(\"warning: CAW_GH_WEBHOOK_SECRET is empty; all webhooks will be rejected\")\n\t}\n\n\tsseHub := sse.New()\n\tengine := settle.New(st, sseHub, settle.DefaultGrace)\n\tr := server.New(st, sseHub, engine, []byte(cfg.GitHubWebhookSecret))\n\n\tsrv := &http.Server{\n\t\tAddr:              cfg.Addr,\n\t\tHandler:           r,\n\t\tReadHeaderTimeout: 10 * time.Second,\n\t}\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"hub listening on %s\", cfg.Addr)\n\t\tif err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) {\n\t\t\tlog.Fatalf(\"listen: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\tctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)\n\tdefer stop()\n\t<-ctx.Done()\n\n\tlog.Println(\"shutting down\")\n\tshutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\tif err := srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"shutdown: %v\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\n// mintToken creates and stores an installation token, printing the raw value\n// (shown once). Args: <installation_id> [org].\nfunc mintToken(st *store.Store, args []string) error {\n\tif len(args) < 1 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"usage: hub mint-token <installation_id> [org]\")\n\t}\n\torg := \"\"\n\tif len(args) > 1 {\n\t\torg = args[1]\n\t}\n\traw, hash, err := auth.GenerateToken()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif err := st.InsertToken(hash, args[0], org); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tfmt.Println(raw)\n\treturn nil\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/internal/server/server_test.go",
  "content": "package server_test\n\nimport (\n\t\"bufio\"\n\t\"bytes\"\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"crypto/hmac\"\n\t\"crypto/sha256\"\n\t\"encoding/hex\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"net/http/httptest\"\n\t\"path/filepath\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/auth\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/server\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/settle\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/sse\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store\"\n)\n\nfunc init() { gin.SetMode(gin.TestMode) }\n\nconst secret = \"itest\"\n\n// checkSuiteFailure is a check_suite=completed/failure payload for o/r#1@sha1.\nvar checkSuiteFailure = []byte(`{\n\t\"action\":\"completed\",\n\t\"check_suite\":{\"id\":99,\"head_sha\":\"sha1\",\"status\":\"completed\",\"conclusion\":\"failure\",\"app\":{\"name\":\"CI\"},\"pull_requests\":[{\"number\":1}]},\n\t\"repository\":{\"name\":\"r\",\"owner\":{\"login\":\"o\"}}\n}`)\n\nfunc newTestServer(t *testing.T, grace time.Duration) (*httptest.Server, string) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tst, err := store.Open(filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), \"it.db\"))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"open store: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tt.Cleanup(func() { _ = st.Close() })\n\n\traw, hash, err := auth.GenerateToken()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"token: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := st.InsertToken(hash, \"inst1\", \"org1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"insert token: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tsseHub := sse.New()\n\tengine := settle.New(st, sseHub, grace)\n\tts := httptest.NewServer(server.New(st, sseHub, engine, []byte(secret)))\n\tt.Cleanup(ts.Close)\n\treturn ts, raw\n}\n\nfunc sign(payload []byte) string {\n\tmac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(secret))\n\tmac.Write(payload)\n\treturn \"sha256=\" + hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil))\n}\n\nfunc postWebhook(t *testing.T, url, event, delivery string, body []byte) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\treq, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, url+\"/webhooks/github\", bytes.NewReader(body))\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-Hub-Signature-256\", sign(body))\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-GitHub-Event\", event)\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-GitHub-Delivery\", delivery)\n\tresp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"post webhook: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusAccepted {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"webhook status = %d, want 202\", resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestEndToEnd_WebhookToSSE: a subscribed Session receives the compiled summary\n// pushed after the Round settles.\nfunc TestEndToEnd_WebhookToSSE(t *testing.T) {\n\tts, token := newTestServer(t, 30*time.Millisecond)\n\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\treq, _ := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, ts.URL+\"/sse/o/r/1\", nil)\n\treq.Header.Set(\"Authorization\", \"Bearer \"+token)\n\tresp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"open sse: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sse status = %d, want 200\", resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n\t// Subscription is registered (headers flushed). Now trigger a settle.\n\tpostWebhook(t, ts.URL, \"check_suite\", \"d1\", checkSuiteFailure)\n\n\tdata := \"\"\n\tsc := bufio.NewScanner(resp.Body)\n\tfor sc.Scan() {\n\t\tif line := sc.Text(); strings.HasPrefix(line, \"data:\") {\n\t\t\tdata = strings.TrimPrefix(line, \"data:\")\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif data == \"\" {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"no SSE data event received before timeout\")\n\t}\n\tvar summary struct {\n\t\tKey    string `json:\"key\"`\n\t\tGroups []struct {\n\t\t\tType    string   `json:\"type\"`\n\t\t\tSources []string `json:\"sources\"`\n\t\t} `json:\"groups\"`\n\t}\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(data), &summary); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"decode summary %q: %v\", data, err)\n\t}\n\tif summary.Key != \"o/r#1@sha1\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"summary key = %q, want o/r#1@sha1\", summary.Key)\n\t}\n\tif len(summary.Groups) != 1 || summary.Groups[0].Type != \"checks\" || summary.Groups[0].Sources[0] != \"CI\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"summary groups = %+v, want one checks/CI group\", summary.Groups)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestEndToEnd_OrphanToPending: with no listener, the summary lands in pending\n// and get_pending returns it.\nfunc TestEndToEnd_OrphanToPending(t *testing.T) {\n\tts, token := newTestServer(t, 15*time.Millisecond)\n\n\tpostWebhook(t, ts.URL, \"check_suite\", \"d1\", checkSuiteFailure)\n\n\tdeadline := time.Now().Add(5 * time.Second)\n\tvar items []store.PendingItem\n\tfor time.Now().Before(deadline) {\n\t\titems = getPending(t, ts.URL, token)\n\t\tif len(items) > 0 {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\ttime.Sleep(20 * time.Millisecond)\n\t}\n\tif len(items) != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"pending items = %d, want 1\", len(items))\n\t}\n\tif items[0].SignalType != \"checks\" || items[0].SHA != \"sha1\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"pending item = %+v\", items[0])\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestPendingAuth: get_pending requires a valid installation token.\nfunc TestPendingAuth(t *testing.T) {\n\tts, token := newTestServer(t, time.Second)\n\n\tcases := []struct {\n\t\tname   string\n\t\theader string\n\t\twant   int\n\t}{\n\t\t{\"no token\", \"\", http.StatusUnauthorized},\n\t\t{\"bad token\", \"Bearer not-a-real-token\", http.StatusUnauthorized},\n\t\t{\"valid token\", \"Bearer \" + token, http.StatusOK},\n\t}\n\tfor _, tc := range cases {\n\t\tt.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {\n\t\t\treq, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, ts.URL+\"/pending\", nil)\n\t\t\tif tc.header != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\treq.Header.Set(\"Authorization\", tc.header)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tresp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"get: %v\", err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t_ = resp.Body.Close()\n\t\t\tif resp.StatusCode != tc.want {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"status = %d, want %d\", resp.StatusCode, tc.want)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc getPending(t *testing.T, url, token string) []store.PendingItem {\n\tt.Helper()\n\treq, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, url+\"/pending\", nil)\n\treq.Header.Set(\"Authorization\", \"Bearer \"+token)\n\tresp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"get pending: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"get pending status = %d\", resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n\tvar body struct {\n\t\tItems []store.PendingItem `json:\"items\"`\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&body); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"decode pending: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn body.Items\n}\n"
}

1	package auth

3	import (
4		"errors"

9		"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
10	)
11	
12	// fakeVerifier is a test double for the Verifier interface. It returns its
13	// configured values regardless of the hash it is given, which is sufficient to
14	// exercise every branch of Required.
15	type fakeVerifier struct {
16		installationID string
17		ok             bool
18		err            error
19	}
20	
21	func (f fakeVerifier) VerifyToken(hash string) (string, bool, error) {
22		return f.installationID, f.ok, f.err
23	}
24	
25	func TestGenerateToken(t *testing.T) {
26		raw1, hash1, err := GenerateToken()
27		if err != nil {
28			t.Fatalf("GenerateToken returned error: %v", err)
29		}
30		if raw1 == "" || hash1 == "" {
31			t.Fatalf("GenerateToken returned empty values: raw=%q hash=%q", raw1, hash1)
32		}
33		if raw1 == hash1 {
34			t.Fatalf("raw and hash should differ, both = %q", raw1)
35		}
36	
37		// HashToken(raw) must reproduce the hash, and must be deterministic.
38		if got := HashToken(raw1); got != hash1 {
39			t.Fatalf("HashToken(raw) = %q, want %q", got, hash1)
40		}
41		if got := HashToken(raw1); got != hash1 {
42			t.Fatalf("HashToken not deterministic: %q != %q", got, hash1)
43		}
44	
45		// A hex SHA-256 is 64 lowercase hex chars.
46		if len(hash1) != 64 {
47			t.Fatalf("hash length = %d, want 64", len(hash1))
48		}
49	
50		// Distinct invocations must yield distinct tokens.
51		raw2, hash2, err := GenerateToken()
52		if err != nil {
53			t.Fatalf("second GenerateToken returned error: %v", err)
54		}
55		if raw1 == raw2 {
56			t.Fatalf("two GenerateToken calls produced identical raw tokens: %q", raw1)
57		}
58		if hash1 == hash2 {
59			t.Fatalf("two GenerateToken calls produced identical hashes: %q", hash1)
60		}
61	}
62	
63	func TestRequired(t *testing.T) {
64		gin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)
65	
66		const wantID = "inst-123"
67	
68		type header struct {
69			key, value string
70		}
71	
72		tests := []struct {
73			name        string
74			verifier    Verifier
75			headers     []header
76			wantStatus  int
77			wantBody    string // non-empty only when the next handler should run
78			wantContext bool
79		}{
80			{
81				name:        "valid bearer token",
82				verifier:    fakeVerifier{installationID: wantID, ok: true},
83				headers:     []header{{"Authorization", "Bearer some-token"}},
84				wantStatus:  http.StatusOK,
85				wantBody:    wantID,
86				wantContext: true,
87			},
88			{
89				name:        "valid via X-Caw-Token header",
90				verifier:    fakeVerifier{installationID: wantID, ok: true},
91				headers:     []header{{"X-Caw-Token", "some-token"}},
92				wantStatus:  http.StatusOK,
93				wantBody:    wantID,
94				wantContext: true,
95			},
96			{
97				name:       "missing header",
98				verifier:   fakeVerifier{installationID: wantID, ok: true},
99				headers:    nil,
100				wantStatus: http.StatusUnauthorized,
101			},
102			{
103				name:       "empty bearer value falls through to missing",
104				verifier:   fakeVerifier{installationID: wantID, ok: true},
105				headers:    []header{{"Authorization", "Bearer "}},
106				wantStatus: http.StatusUnauthorized,
107			},
108			{
109				name:       "unknown token",
110				verifier:   fakeVerifier{ok: false},
111				headers:    []header{{"Authorization", "Bearer some-token"}},
112				wantStatus: http.StatusUnauthorized,
113			},
114			{
115				name:       "verifier error",
116				verifier:   fakeVerifier{err: errors.New("db down")},
117				headers:    []header{{"Authorization", "Bearer some-token"}},
118				wantStatus: http.StatusInternalServerError,
119			},
120		}
121	
122		for _, tt := range tests {
123			t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
124				var sawContext bool
125	
126				r := gin.New()
127				r.GET("/pending", Required(tt.verifier), func(c *gin.Context) {
128					id, exists := c.Get(ContextInstallationID)
129					sawContext = exists
130					idStr, _ := id.(string)
131					c.String(http.StatusOK, idStr)
132				})
133	
134				req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/pending", nil)
135				for _, h := range tt.headers {
136					req.Header.Set(h.key, h.value)
137				}
138				w := httptest.NewRecorder()
139				r.ServeHTTP(w, req)
140	
141				if w.Code != tt.wantStatus {
142					t.Fatalf("status = %d, want %d", w.Code, tt.wantStatus)
143				}
144				if sawContext != tt.wantContext {
145					t.Fatalf("next-handler ran (context set) = %v, want %v", sawContext, tt.wantContext)
146				}
147				if tt.wantBody != "" && w.Body.String() != tt.wantBody {
148					t.Fatalf("body = %q, want %q", w.Body.String(), tt.wantBody)
149				}
150			})
151		}
152	}
153

### Example YAML Configuration for CodeRabbit

Source: https://docs.coderabbit.ai/getting-started/yaml-configuration

This is a sample `.coderabbit.yaml` file demonstrating various configuration options for CodeRabbit, including language, review settings, and chat preferences. Place this file in the root of your repository.

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://coderabbit.ai/integrations/schema.v2.json
language: "en-US"
early_access: false
reviews:
  profile: "chill"
  request_changes_workflow: false
  high_level_summary: true
  poem: true
  review_status: true
  review_details: false
  auto_review:
    enabled: true
    drafts: false
chat:
  auto_reply: true
```

--------------------------------

### CodeRabbit Configuration for Django

Source: https://docs.coderabbit.ai/configuration/example/python/django

This YAML configuration defines settings for CodeRabbit integration within a Django project. It specifies language, review preferences, and detailed instructions for analyzing Python files and test files.

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://coderabbit.ai/integrations/schema.v2.json
language: "en-US"
early_access: false
reviews:
  profile: "chill"
  request_changes_workflow: false
  high_level_summary: true
  poem: true
  review_status: true
  collapse_walkthrough: false
  auto_review:
    enabled: true
    drafts: false
  path_instructions:
    - path: "**/*.py"
      instructions: |
        Review the following code using Python Django. Ensure that:
          - Ensure each method has a clear, detailed docstring explaining its purpose, parameters, and return values. Enhance existing docstrings for clarity and completeness.
          - Do not suggest adding type annotations.
          - Review Django querysets for potential performance improvements and suggest optimizations where applicable.
          - Ensure that for every model.save() there is a model.full_clean() that precedes it.
          - Avoid adding generic exception handling (e.g., `except Exception`) in test files. Each test failure should be addressed by the developer with specific exception handling or fixes.
          - Avoid adding comments relating to importing modules.
    - path: "tests/*.py"
      instructions: |
        - There is no sensitive information in the test files. All data handled is procedural/generic and non-sensitive.
chat:
  auto_reply: true

```

--------------------------------

### Auto Review Configuration Example

Source: https://docs.coderabbit.ai/configuration/auto-review

A complete example of the auto_review configuration in YAML format.

```yaml
reviews:
  auto_review:
    enabled: true
    auto_incremental_review: true
    auto_pause_after_reviewed_commits: 10
    drafts: false
    base_branches:
      - "develop"
      - "release/.*"
    ignore_title_keywords:
      - "WIP"
      - "[skip review]"
    labels:
      - "!do-not-review"
    ignore_usernames:
      - "dependabot[bot]"
      - "renovate[bot]"
      - "release-bot"
    description_keyword: ""
```

### YAML Configuration for Mintlify Documentation

Source: https://docs.coderabbit.ai/configuration/example/other/mintlify-documentation

This is the main configuration file for CodeRabbit's documentation generation. It specifies the theme, language, review profiles, and path filters to control how documentation is processed and reviewed.

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://coderabbit.ai/integrations/schema.v2.json
language: "en-US"
reviews:
  profile: "chill"
  request_changes_workflow: false
  high_level_summary: true
  poem: false
  review_status: true
  finishing_touches:
    docstrings:
      enabled: false
  path_filters:
    - "!**/node_modules/**"
    - "!**/.github/**"
    - "!**/.vscode/**"
    - "!**/pnpm-lock.yaml"
    - "!**/package-lock.json"
    - "!**/images/**"
    - "!**/*.svg"
    - "!**/*.png"
    - "!openapi.json"
  path_instructions:
    - path: "**/*.mdx"
      instructions: |
        - When reviewing MDX files, check for duplicated content across documentation pages. If rate limits, feature descriptions, or other factual information is updated in one file, verify if similar or duplicated content exists in other MDX files that may also need updating. Raise a warning if potentially related content is found in other files but not updated.
        - CodeRabbit can be configured via web interface or via `.coderabbit.yaml` configuration file. In the documentation, both ways should be explained using the Mintlify Tabs component. Good Example: 
          ```
          <Tabs>
            <Tab title="Using configuration file">
              ```yaml
              chat:
                integrations:
                  jira:
                    usage: enabled
              ```
            </Tab>
            <Tab title="Using the Web Interface">
              ![Jira configuration](images/assets/images/jira-integration-setup.png)
            </Tab>
          </Tabs>
          ```
        - Recommend using tabs for other cases when the content is a good fit. For example, when showing mutually exclusive instructions (windows/linux, jira/linear, etc.)
        - Every time a page (.mdx file) is created, renamed, or deleted, the `/docs.json` file should be checked for needed adjustments. Example:
          ```
          File `management/seats-licenses-billing-faq.mdx` was renamed to `management/seat-assignment.mdx`. In this case, it should be also adjusted in `docs.json`. If the file existed for some time already with the old name, a redirect should be added accordingly.
          ```
        - For Pro plan feature warnings, check if inline Info, Warning or Tip blocks mentioning "Pro plan" and "pricing page" are used. If found, recommend replacing them with the ProPlanInfo snippet: `import ProPlanInfo from "/snippets/ProPlanInfo.mdx"` and `<ProPlanInfo />` instead of inline Info blocks.
    - path: "docs.json"
      instructions: |
        Check that all referenced files exist. Flag any broken file references.
    - path: "changelog.mdx"
      instructions: |
        Verify changelog entries follow consistent formatting: date, clear title, and include relevant links. Check that new entries are added chronologically.

```

### Squawk GitHub Action Workflow

Source: https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk/blob/master/docs/docs/github_app.md

This YAML workflow demonstrates how to use the squawk-action to lint modified SQL migrations on pull requests. It checks out the code, finds modified migrations, and then runs the squawk-action with the identified patterns.

```yaml
# .github/workflows/lint-migrations.yml
name: Lint Migrations

on: pull_request

jobs:
  lint_migrations:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      - name: Find modified migrations
        run: |
          modified_migrations=$(git diff --name-only origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF...origin/$GITHUB_HEAD_REF 'migrations/*.sql')
          echo "$modified_migrations"
          echo "::set-output name=file_names::$modified_migrations"
        id: modified-migrations
      - uses: sbdchd/squawk-action@v1
        with:
          pattern: ${{ steps.modified-migrations.outputs.file_names }}

```

--------------------------------

### Running Squawk Upload to GitHub

Source: https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk/blob/master/docs/docs/github_app.md

This command demonstrates how to execute the Squawk CLI to upload a SQL file as a comment to a GitHub pull request, assuming the necessary environment variables are already set.

```bash
squawk upload-to-github example.sql
```

--------------------------------

### Custom GitHub Action for Squawk Upload

Source: https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk/blob/master/docs/docs/github_app.md

This bash script shows how to manually call Squawk to upload comments to GitHub from a custom GitHub Action. It sets up necessary environment variables for the GitHub token, repository owner and name, and the pull request number before executing the squawk upload-to-github command.

```bash
SQUAWK_GITHUB_TOKEN=${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
SQUAWK_GITHUB_REPO_OWNER=$(echo $GITHUB_REPOSITORY | awk -F/ '{print $1}')
SQUAWK_GITHUB_REPO_NAME=$(echo $GITHUB_REPOSITORY | awk -F/ '{print $2}')
SQUAWK_GITHUB_PR_NUMBER=$(echo $GITHUB_REF | awk 'BEGIN { FS = "/" } ; { print $3 }')
squawk upload-to-github example.sql

```

--------------------------------

Source: https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk/blob/master/README.md

Provides an example of a `.squawk.toml` configuration file, demonstrating how to define excluded rules. This file allows for project-wide rule configurations.

```toml
excluded_rules = [
    "require-concurrent-index-creation",
    "require-concurrent-index-deletion",
]
```

### Custom SQLFluff Configuration

Source: https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/gettingstarted.html

Create a `.sqlfluff` file to define custom indentation and keyword capitalization rules. This configuration is placed in the same directory as the SQL file to be linted.

```ini
[sqlfluff]
dialect = ansi

[sqlfluff:indentation]
tab_space_size = 2

[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.keywords]
capitalisation_policy = lower

```

--------------------------------

### lint

Source: https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/reference/api.html

Lints a SQL string to identify violations. It supports specifying the dialect, rules to lint for or exclude, and configuration options via objects or file paths.

```APIDOC
## lint

### Description
Lints a SQL string to identify violations.

### Method
`lint`

### Parameters
- **sql** (str) - Required - The SQL to be linted.
- **dialect** (Optional[str]) - Optional - A reference to the dialect of the SQL to be linted. Defaults to ansi.
- **rules** (Optional[list[str]]) - Optional - A list of rule references to lint for. Defaults to None.
- **exclude_rules** (Optional[list[str]]) - Optional - A list of rule references to avoid linting for. Defaults to None.
- **config** (Optional[FluffConfig]) - Optional - A configuration object to use for the operation. Defaults to None.
- **config_path** (Optional[str]) - Optional - A path to a .sqlfluff config, which is only used if a config is not already provided. Defaults to None.

### Returns
`list[dict[str, Any]]` for each violation found.
```

--------------------------------

### Lint SQL Files with Specific Rules and Dialect

Source: https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/reference/cli.html

Lints a specific SQL file, applying only the listed rules for the given dialect. Useful for targeted code reviews.

```bash
sqlfluff lint --dialect mysql --rules ST05 my_query.sql
```

--------------------------------

### SQLFluff Configuration File Example

Source: https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/configuration/setting_configuration.html

Example of a SQLFluff configuration file showing settings for capitalisation and not equal convention.

```ini
[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.keywords]
capitalisation_policy = lower
[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.identifiers]
extended_capitalisation_policy = lower
[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.functions]
extended_capitalisation_policy = lower
[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.literals]
capitalisation_policy = lower
[sqlfluff:rules:capitalisation.types]
extended_capitalisation_policy = lower

# The default configuration for the not equal convention rule is "consistent"
# which will auto-detect the setting from the rest of the file.
# This is less desirable in a new project and you may find this (slightly
# more strict) setting more useful.
[sqlfluff:rules:convention.not_equal]
# Default to preferring the "c_style" (i.e. `!=`)
preferred_not_equal_style = c_style

```

### Getting Started > Custom Usage

Source: https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/gettingstarted.html

SQLFluff allows customization of its behavior to match diverse SQL styling conventions. This is achieved by creating a configuration file named `.sqlfluff` in the same directory as the SQL file. This file can specify settings such as the dialect, indentation tab space size, and keyword capitalization policies.
```

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01KymKu3mQQCT4SduZbvtBbH
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/.sqlfluff",
  "content": "# SQLFluff config. Root dialect is sqlite (the Hub's embedded store, ADR-0001).\n# When Postgres migrations land, add migrations/postgres/.sqlfluff with\n# `dialect = postgres` — the nearest config to a file wins.\n[sqlfluff]\ndialect = sqlite\nmax_line_length = 120\n\n# Column definitions in our DDL are intentionally aligned for readability;\n# layout.spacing (LT01) fights that and is style, not correctness.\nexclude_rules = layout.spacing\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Quick answer to your question, then I'll keep building:

**We are using Gin** — for the Hub's inbound HTTP server. `internal/server/server.go` is `gin.New()` with the webhook, SSE, `/pending`, and `/healthz` routes; `cmd/hub` runs it. That hasn't changed.

What you may be noticing: the Slice 3 mergeability **poller** makes an **outbound** call to GitHub's REST API (`GET /pulls/{n}`). Gin is a *server* framework (it routes requests coming *in*); it's not an HTTP *client*. Outbound calls use the stdlib `net/http` client — that's what you'd use under any framework (even GitHub's official Go SDK is just a wrapper over `net/http`). So: Gin for serving, `net/http` for calling out. No framework switch.

Now continuing Slice 3 — config, the GitHub client, and the mergeability poller:

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/internal/ghclient/client.go",
  "content": "// Package ghclient is a minimal GitHub REST client for the Hub's outbound\n// calls (the mergeability poll — the one poll in the system). It is a plain\n// net/http client; Gin is the inbound server framework and does not apply here.\npackage ghclient\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\nconst defaultBaseURL = \"https://api.github.com\"\n\n// Client calls the GitHub REST API.\ntype Client struct {\n\thttpClient *http.Client\n\tbaseURL    string\n\ttoken      string\n}\n\n// New returns a Client. An empty baseURL defaults to api.github.com; an empty\n// token sends unauthenticated requests.\nfunc New(baseURL, token string) *Client {\n\tif baseURL == \"\" {\n\t\tbaseURL = defaultBaseURL\n\t}\n\treturn &Client{\n\t\thttpClient: &http.Client{Timeout: 15 * time.Second},\n\t\tbaseURL:    baseURL,\n\t\ttoken:      token,\n\t}\n}\n\n// PullState is the subset of GET /pulls/{n} the Hub uses for mergeability.\ntype PullState struct {\n\tState          string `json:\"state\"`\n\tMergeable      *bool  `json:\"mergeable\"`\n\tMergeableState string `json:\"mergeable_state\"`\n}\n\n// PullMergeability fetches a PR's mergeability.\nfunc (c *Client) PullMergeability(ctx context.Context, owner, repo string, number int) (PullState, error) {\n\turl := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s/repos/%s/%s/pulls/%d\", c.baseURL, owner, repo, number)\n\treq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, url, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PullState{}, fmt.Errorf(\"build request: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treq.Header.Set(\"Accept\", \"application/vnd.github+json\")\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-GitHub-Api-Version\", \"2022-11-28\")\n\tif c.token != \"\" {\n\t\treq.Header.Set(\"Authorization\", \"Bearer \"+c.token)\n\t}\n\n\tresp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PullState{}, fmt.Errorf(\"get pull: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\treturn PullState{}, fmt.Errorf(\"get pull %s/%s#%d: status %d\", owner, repo, number, resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n\n\tvar ps PullState\n\tif err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&ps); err != nil {\n\t\treturn PullState{}, fmt.Errorf(\"decode pull: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn ps, nil\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/caw/internal/mergeability/poller.go",
  "content": "// Package mergeability turns a GitHub mergeability poll into a Hub signal.\n// It is the one poll in the system, run after a Round settles (CONTEXT.md).\npackage mergeability\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/ghclient\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store\"\n)\n\n// Poller adapts a GitHub client to settle.MergeabilityPoller.\ntype Poller struct {\n\tclient  *ghclient.Client\n\ttimeout time.Duration\n}\n\n// New returns a Poller backed by c.\nfunc New(c *ghclient.Client) *Poller {\n\treturn &Poller{client: c, timeout: 15 * time.Second}\n}\n\n// Mergeability polls the PR and returns the mergeability signal for the Round.\n// A stable ExternalID means a re-poll replaces the prior signal (ADR-0006).\nfunc (p *Poller) Mergeability(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) (store.Signal, bool, error) {\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), p.timeout)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\n\tps, err := p.client.PullMergeability(ctx, owner, repo, number)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn store.Signal{}, false, err\n\t}\n\tseverity, body := classify(ps)\n\treturn store.Signal{\n\t\tOwner: owner, Repo: repo, Number: number, SHA: sha,\n\t\tSignalType: \"mergeability\", Source: \"poll\", ExternalID: \"mergeability\",\n\t\tSeverity: severity, Body: body,\n\t}, true, nil\n}\n\n// classify maps GitHub's mergeable_state to a severity + human body.\nfunc classify(ps ghclient.PullState) (severity, body string) {\n\tswitch ps.MergeableState {\n\tcase \"clean\":\n\t\treturn \"\", \"clean\"\n\tcase \"behind\":\n\t\treturn \"MINOR\", \"behind base\"\n\tcase \"dirty\":\n\t\treturn \"MAJOR\", \"conflicts\"\n\tcase \"blocked\":\n\t\treturn \"MINOR\", \"blocked\"\n\tcase \"unstable\":\n\t\treturn \"MINOR\", \"unstable (failing required checks)\"\n\tcase \"\", \"unknown\":\n\t\treturn \"\", \"unknown\"\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn \"\", ps.MergeableState\n\t}\n}\n"
}

1	// Package settle schedules Round settles: a grace window after a trigger,
2	// re-armed by late same-SHA signals (re-settle, ADR-0004). On fire it compiles
3	// the Round's signals into one summary and fans it out, or stores it as pending
4	// when no Session is listening (ADR-0006).
5	package settle
6	
7	import (
8		"encoding/json"
9		"fmt"
10		"log"
11		"sync"
12		"time"
13	
14		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/compile"
15		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store"
16	)
17	
18	// DefaultGrace is the settle grace window after the latest trigger.
19	const DefaultGrace = 30 * time.Second
20	
21	// Publisher fans a serialized summary out to subscribers of a PR key.
22	type Publisher interface {
23		Publish(key string, msg []byte) int
24	}
25	
26	// Engine schedules and fires Round settles.
27	type Engine struct {
28		store *store.Store
29		pub   Publisher
30		grace time.Duration
31	
32		mu     sync.Mutex
33		rounds map[string]*roundState
34	}
35	
36	type roundState struct {
37		owner, repo string
38		number      int
39		sha         string
40		timer       *time.Timer
41		seq         int
42	}
43	
44	// New builds an Engine. A non-positive grace falls back to DefaultGrace.
45	func New(st *store.Store, pub Publisher, grace time.Duration) *Engine {
46		if grace <= 0 {
47			grace = DefaultGrace
48		}
49		return &Engine{store: st, pub: pub, grace: grace, rounds: make(map[string]*roundState)}
50	}
51	
52	func roundKey(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) string {
53		return fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s#%d@%s", owner, repo, number, sha)
54	}
55	
56	// PRKey is the SSE subscription key for a PR (owner/repo#number), independent of SHA.
57	func PRKey(owner, repo string, number int) string {
58		return fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s#%d", owner, repo, number)
59	}
60	
61	// Touch (re)arms the grace timer for a Round. The first call opens the settle;
62	// a later same-SHA call re-settles it (ADR-0004).
63	func (e *Engine) Touch(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) {
64		rk := roundKey(owner, repo, number, sha)
65		e.mu.Lock()
66		defer e.mu.Unlock()
67		rs := e.rounds[rk]
68		if rs == nil {
69			rs = &roundState{owner: owner, repo: repo, number: number, sha: sha}
70			e.rounds[rk] = rs
71		}
72		if rs.timer != nil {
73			rs.timer.Stop()
74		}
75		rs.timer = time.AfterFunc(e.grace, func() { e.fire(rk) })
76	}
77	
78	// FireNow settles a Round immediately, bypassing the grace timer.
79	func (e *Engine) FireNow(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) {
80		rk := roundKey(owner, repo, number, sha)
81		e.mu.Lock()
82		rs := e.rounds[rk]
83		if rs == nil {
84			rs = &roundState{owner: owner, repo: repo, number: number, sha: sha}
85			e.rounds[rk] = rs
86		}
87		if rs.timer != nil {
88			rs.timer.Stop()
89		}
90		e.mu.Unlock()
91		e.fire(rk)
92	}
93	
94	// fire compiles and dispatches a settle for the given Round.
95	func (e *Engine) fire(rk string) {
96		e.mu.Lock()
97		rs := e.rounds[rk]
98		if rs == nil {
99			e.mu.Unlock()
100			return
101		}
102		rs.seq++
103		seq := rs.seq
104		owner, repo, number, sha := rs.owner, rs.repo, rs.number, rs.sha
105		e.mu.Unlock()
106	
107		signals, err := e.store.SignalsForRound(owner, repo, number, sha)
108		if err != nil {
109			log.Printf("settle %s: load signals: %v", rk, err)
110			return
111		}
112		summary := compile.Compile(rk, seq, signals)
113		msg, err := json.Marshal(summary)
114		if err != nil {
115			log.Printf("settle %s: marshal: %v", rk, err)
116			return
117		}
118	
119		if e.pub.Publish(PRKey(owner, repo, number), msg) == 0 {
120			// Orphaned: no live Session. Persist as pending, latest per signal-type.
121			for _, g := range summary.Groups {
122				if err := e.store.UpsertPending(store.PendingItem{
123					Owner: owner, Repo: repo, Number: number,
124					SignalType: g.Type, SHA: sha, PRState: "open", Summary: string(msg),
125				}); err != nil {
126					log.Printf("settle %s: pend %s: %v", rk, g.Type, err)
127				}
128			}
129		}
130	}
131	
```

It returns a signal to fold into the summary;\n// ok=false skips it.\ntype MergeabilityPoller interface {\n\tMergeability(owner, repo string, number int, sha string) (store.Signal, bool, error)\n}\n\n// Option configures an Engine.\ntype Option func(*Engine)\n\n// WithPoller wires a mergeability poller invoked at each settle.\nfunc WithPoller(p MergeabilityPoller) Option {\n\treturn func(e *Engine) { e.poller = p }\n}\n\n// Engine schedules and fires Round settles.\ntype Engine struct {\n\tstore  *store.Store\n\tpub    Publisher\n\tgrace  time.Duration\n\tpoller MergeabilityPoller // optional; nil disables the mergeability poll\n\n\tmu     sync.Mutex\n\trounds map[string]*roundState\n}"

1	// Command hub is the Caw Hub: it ingests GitHub webhooks, compiles PR feedback
2	// into summaries, fans them out to Watchers over SSE, and stores orphaned
3	// feedback as pending items.
4	//
5	// Subcommand: `hub mint-token <installation_id> [org]` prints a new raw token.
6	package main
7	
8	import (
9		"context"
10		"errors"
11		"fmt"
12		"log"
13		"net/http"
14		"os"
15		"os/signal"
16		"syscall"
17		"time"
18	
19		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/auth"
20		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/config"
21		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/server"
22		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/settle"
23		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/sse"
24		"github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/internal/store"
25	)
26	
27	func main() {
28		cfg := config.Load()
29	
30		st, err := store.Open(cfg.DatabasePath)
31		if err != nil {
32			log.Fatalf("open store: %v", err)
33		}
34		defer func() { _ = st.Close() }()
35	
36		if len(os.Args) > 1 && os.Args[1] == "mint-token" {
37			if err := mintToken(st, os.Args[2:]); err != nil {
38				log.Fatalf("mint-token: %v", err)
39			}
40			return
41		}
42	
43		if cfg.GitHubWebhookSecret == "" {
44			log.Println("warning: CAW_GH_WEBHOOK_SECRET is empty; all webhooks will be rejected")
45		}
46	
47		sseHub := sse.New()
48		engine := settle.New(st, sseHub, settle.DefaultGrace)
49		r := server.New(st, sseHub, engine, []byte(cfg.GitHubWebhookSecret))
50	
51		srv := &http.Server{
52			Addr:              cfg.Addr,
53			Handler:           r,
54			ReadHeaderTimeout: 10 * time.Second,
55		}
56	
57		go func() {
58			log.Printf("hub listening on %s", cfg.Addr)
59			if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) {
60				log.Fatalf("listen: %v", err)
61			}
62		}()
63	
64		ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)
65		defer stop()
66		<-ctx.Done()
67	
68		log.Println("shutting down")
69		shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
70		defer cancel()
71		if err := srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx); err != nil {
72			log.Printf("shutdown: %v", err)
73		}
74	}
75	
76	// mintToken creates and stores an installation token, printing the raw value
77	// (shown once). Args: <installation_id> [org].
78	func mintToken(st *store.Store, args []string) error {
79		if len(args) < 1 {
80			return fmt.Errorf("usage: hub mint-token <installation_id> [org]")
81		}
82		org := ""
83		if len(args) > 1 {
84			org = args[1]
85		}
86		raw, hash, err := auth.GenerateToken()
87		if err != nil {
88			return err
89		}
90		if err := st.InsertToken(hash, args[0], org); err != nil {
91			return err
92		}
93		fmt.Println(raw)
94		return nil
95	}
96

> AGENT

**We are using Gin** — for the entire inbound HTTP layer. Concretely, on `main` right now:

- `internal/server/server.go` → `gin.New()`, `gin.Logger()/Recovery()`, and every route:
  - `POST /webhooks/github`, `GET /healthz`, `GET /pending`, `GET /sse/:owner/:repo/:number`
- `internal/hub/webhook.go` → handlers are `gin.HandlerFunc` over `*gin.Context`
- `internal/sse/handler.go` → SSE streaming via Gin's `c.Stream`
- `internal/auth/token.go` → the auth middleware is Gin

The **only** file using stdlib `net/http` is `internal/ghclient/client.go`, and that's correct: it's an **outbound** client calling GitHub's API (`GET /pulls/{n}` for the mergeability poll). Gin is a *server* framework — it routes requests coming **in**; it has no HTTP *client*. So you can't "use Gin" for an outbound call regardless of preference.

If your actual question is **"why hand-roll the GitHub call instead of using a library like `google/go-github`?"** — that's a fair one. I kept it as a ~40-line `net/http` client because we touch exactly one endpoint and one field set; pulling in the full SDK (and its deps) for that felt heavier than warranted, and Slice 5 will swap the auth to App installation tokens anyway. **Happy to switch `ghclient` to `google/go-github` if you'd prefer the SDK** — say the word.

---

=== Issue #1 ===
state=OPEN
---
**Goal:** A tracer-bullet Hub that ingests a verified GitHub webhook end-to-end and buckets it into a Round persisted in SQLite.

## Scope
- `POST /webhooks/github` endpoint that verifies `X-Hub-Signature-256` (HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body) and rejects bad/missing signatures.
- Webhook **dedupe** (e.g. by GitHub delivery id) so a redelivered event is ingested once.
- **Round bucketing**: key each event by `owner/repo#number @ sha`; one Round per PR head SHA.
- SQLite **pending store skeleton**: schema + write path for Round/event rows (latest-state-per-signal-type shape, no compile yet).
- Single portable Go service per ADR-0001 (embedded SQLite, runs identically self-host or SaaS).

## Acceptance
- A request with a valid `X-Hub-Signature-256` is accepted (2xx); an invalid or missing signature is rejected (4xx) and not stored.
- A redelivered webhook (same delivery id) does not create a duplicate row.
- Two events for the same `owner/repo#number @ sha` land in the same Round; a different SHA opens a new Round.
- Ingested events are persisted to SQLite and survive a process restart.

## Refs
ADR: `docs/adr/0001-portable-go-sqlite-hub-over-cloudflare.md`, `docs/adr/0006-pending-is-latest-state-per-type-consumer-owns-relevance.md`. CONTEXT: **Round**, **Signal-type**, **Pending item**.

> AGENT

The audit surfaced **two real, never-reviewed Slice-1 issues**:

1. **Dead/duplicated code** — `DeriveRound`/`RoundKey` in `internal/hub/round.go` are used *only by tests*; Slice 2's `ingest` reimplemented round derivation and stopped calling them. Two divergent code paths = drift risk.
2. **Dedupe drops events on failure** — `HandleWebhook` claims the delivery id (`SeenDelivery`) *before* processing. If `ingest` later errors (500), GitHub redelivers the **same** id → we now answer "duplicate" (200) and the event is **silently lost**. That's a correctness bug.

Let me fix both on a branch. First the branch and reading current files:

  "old_string": "// HandleWebhook is the POST /webhooks/github handler: verify, dedupe, then ingest.\nfunc (h *Hub) HandleWebhook(c *gin.Context) {\n\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(c.Request.Body, maxBody))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"read body\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif len(h.secret) == 0 || !VerifySignature(h.secret, body, c.GetHeader(\"X-Hub-Signature-256\")) {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusUnauthorized, \"invalid signature\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tevent := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Event\")\n\tif delivery := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Delivery\"); delivery != \"\" {\n\t\tisNew, err := h.store.SeenDelivery(delivery, event)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"dedupe delivery %s: %v\", delivery, err)\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"dedupe\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !isNew {\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusOK, \"duplicate\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tvar env github.Envelope\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(body, &env); err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"parse payload\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.ingest(event, env); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"ingest %s: %v\", event, err)\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"ingest\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tc.String(http.StatusAccepted, \"accepted\")\n}",
  "new_string": "// HandleWebhook is the POST /webhooks/github handler: verify the signature,\n// dedupe by delivery id, ingest, and only THEN record the delivery as processed.\n//\n// Recording after ingest (not before) is deliberate: if ingest fails we return\n// 5xx with the delivery un-recorded, so GitHub's redelivery of the same id is\n// reprocessed rather than silently dropped as a \"duplicate\". ingest is\n// idempotent (upserts), so a redelivery race is harmless.\nfunc (h *Hub) HandleWebhook(c *gin.Context) {\n\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(c.Request.Body, maxBody))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"read body\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif len(h.secret) == 0 || !VerifySignature(h.secret, body, c.GetHeader(\"X-Hub-Signature-256\")) {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusUnauthorized, \"invalid signature\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tevent := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Event\")\n\tdelivery := c.GetHeader(\"X-GitHub-Delivery\")\n\tif delivery != \"\" {\n\t\tseen, err := h.store.HasDelivery(delivery)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"dedupe check %s: %v\", delivery, err)\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"dedupe\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif seen {\n\t\t\tc.String(http.StatusOK, \"duplicate\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tvar env github.Envelope\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(body, &env); err != nil {\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusBadRequest, \"parse payload\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.ingest(event, env); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"ingest %s: %v\", event, err)\n\t\tc.String(http.StatusInternalServerError, \"ingest\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif delivery != \"\" {\n\t\tif _, err := h.store.SeenDelivery(delivery, event); err != nil {\n\t\t\t// Non-fatal: a missed mark just risks one idempotent reprocess.\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"record delivery %s: %v\", delivery, err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tc.String(http.StatusAccepted, \"accepted\")\n}"
}

40	// SeenDelivery records a GitHub delivery id and reports whether it is new.
41	// It returns true the first time an id is seen, false for duplicates.
42	func (s *Store) SeenDelivery(id, event string) (bool, error) {
43		res, err := s.db.Exec(
44			`INSERT OR IGNORE INTO deliveries (id, event, received_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)`,
45			id, event, time.Now().Unix(),
46		)
47		if err != nil {
48			return false, fmt.Errorf("record delivery: %w", err)
49		}
50		n, err := res.RowsAffected()
51		if err != nil {
52			return false, fmt.Errorf("delivery rows: %w", err)
53		}
54		return n == 1, nil
55	}
56

> AGENT

Done. Both parts of your question, resolved:

## "Are PRs raised for work so far?"
- **Now yes, for everything.** Slices 2 (#13), 3 (#15), tooling (#14), Dependabot (#9–12), and this fix (#16) all went through PRs + CI + CodeRabbit.
- The gap was **Slice 1**: it (and the early CI/test/codecov commits) landed **directly on `main` before branch protection existed** — never PR'd or reviewed. PR #16 has now retroactively put the Slice-1 code through review.

## "Fix issues in Slice 1"
Audited the unreviewed Slice-1 code, found **two real issues**, fixed both in **[PR #16](https://github.com/ravencloak-org/caw/pull/16) (merged)**:

1. **Correctness — dedupe dropped events on failure.** The handler recorded the delivery id *before* `ingest`; a 5xx made GitHub's redelivery look like a `duplicate` (200) → event silently lost. Now `HasDelivery` gates dups up front and the delivery is marked **only after successful ingest**, so a 5xx lets the redelivery reprocess (ingest is idempotent).
2. **Dead/duplicated code** — `DeriveRound`/`RoundKey` were test-only after Slice 2's `ingest` took over Round derivation. Removed the file + test to prevent drift.

Added `store.HasDelivery` + tests; build/race/lint/sqlfluff all clean.

## State
- **Issue #1: CLOSED** (the handoff's "loose end" is resolved).
- `main @ 3b464da`, green, no open PRs.
- **Remaining issues: #4–#8** (Watcher MCP, GitHub App, Rebase, Severity, Observability).

Note: the handoff doc at `/tmp/caw-handoff.md` now slightly understates progress (it flagged #1 as open). Want me to refresh it, pick up **#4 (Watcher MCP)** next, or do the `google/go-github` SDK swap you flagged earlier?
